Yes, I do feel singled out, and I think people are working together more than they admit. It’s mostly my husband’s family—this has been going on for months. They act pleasant enough when people are looking, especially at church, but then these little things start happening.

So yes, I believe it’s tied back to them and meant to hurt me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people are working together against you or that you are being singled out for special reasons that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do feel singled out, and I think people are working together more than they admit. It’s mostly my husband’s family—this has been going on for months. They act pleasant enough when people are looking, especially at church, but then these little things start happening.\n\nLike at work, with billing errors showing up—small enough that someone can say, “Oh, that’s just a mistake,” but I’m a billing clerk, I know how these things usually look. And then someone at church acts strange toward me, like they’ve heard something about me or about our money, our good name. It all sort of… it all points back to them. People say, “Well, the records don’t show anything,” but records don’t show whispers or people quietly arranging things. So yes, I think they’re targeting me in ways other people don’t understand.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced things that others around you don't seem to notice—like hearing voices, seeing things, or sensing a presence that isn't there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like hearing voices or seeing something that isn’t there. I mean, sometimes I get a feeling when I walk into church that people have been talking, but that’s not a “presence,” exactly—that’s just how people act, you know? You can tell by the way they stop talking or look over.\n\nBut no, I haven’t noticed anything like voices or visions. It’s more the little things people do.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that certain events or actions by others have a special and personal meaning for you that you’re sure others don’t fully understand or recognize?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s what I’m trying to say. These things aren’t just normal little mix-ups. Like the billing errors at work—people act like, “Oh, it happens,” but I know how those systems work. I’ve done that kind of work for years. And then someone at church suddenly gets odd with me, like they know something, or they’ve been told something about our money or our name.\n\nAnd see, my husband’s family has always been… well, I don’t want to get into every family story, but they know how to keep their hands clean while making trouble. So when people say the records don’t show anything, that doesn’t mean much to me. Records don’t show what gets said quietly, or how people make sure you look bad without leaving fingerprints. So yes, I’m sure it means something, and I don’t think other people really understand how it all ties back to them.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yes, I do. I mean, I know how that sounds when you say “without clear evidence,” but that’s the whole point—they don’t do it in a way you can just print out and show somebody.

Well, I wouldn’t call it “strange beliefs,” exactly. That makes it sound like I just woke up one day and decided something out of nowhere. It’s been months of things adding up.

I’m here because I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. Like these little billing errors that show up at work—people say, “Oh, it’s probably just a mistake,” but I know how that kind of thing works. And then someone at church starts acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something, and you know, churches are like small towns, people talk even when they pretend they don’t.

So no, I haven’t heard things other people don’t hear, not like voices. And my thoughts aren’t jumping around—I know it may sound like I’m going off, but it all connects. No matter what records people pull up, I know it ties back to them.

Yes, that’s exactly it. I’ve tried to explain it to people, and they look at me like I’m making too much out of things. But I’m not. For months now I’ve been trying to get people to see that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I mean, it’s been months, definitely more than a month. I’ve been completely convinced because I can see how it all fits together, even if other people act like they can’t.

It’s my husband’s family. They’re quietly messing with our money and our good name. Like at work, these little billing errors show up—small enough that someone can say, “Oh, that’s just a mistake,” but I know billing, I know when something isn’t right. And then at church someone suddenly acts funny toward me, like they’ve heard something, and nobody will come out and say it because people don’t do that directly.

So yes, it’s affected things. I get distracted trying to figure it out, and people think I’m not being myself, but how am I supposed to just ignore it? No matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them.

I don’t know… disconnected from reality sounds a little dramatic to me. I mean, I get overwhelmed sometimes, sure, especially when people keep acting like I’m the one not seeing things right. But it’s not like the room turns fake or I don’t know where I am.

I still go about my day. I know what’s around me. Like at work, when I’m posting payments, you have to stay aware of numbers and names and all that—though honestly, people don’t understand how easy it is for one “little” thing to get overlooked and then suddenly it’s your fault. But no, I wouldn’t say I’ve felt like nothing was real.

I guess I’m not totally sure what you mean by “what was real anymore.” I know what I’ve noticed. Other people just don’t want to see it.

It’s more the little things people do.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that certain events or actions by others have a special and personal meaning for you that you’re sure others don’t fully understand or recognize?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s what I’m trying to say. I mean, I know how that sounds when you say “without clear evidence,” but that’s the whole point—they don’t do it in a way you can just print out and show somebody.\n\nI’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s little things, like billing errors showing up at work—things that look like nothing if you don’t know the system—and then someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me, like they’ve heard something. And my mother-in-law, she’s always been the kind to smile at a potluck and then somehow everyone knows your business by Sunday afternoon.\n\nSo yes, I believe it ties back to them. Even if the records don’t show it, I know what’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a time when you had strange beliefs that others couldn’t understand, heard things that others didn’t hear, or found your thoughts jumping around so much that it was hard to make sense?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn’t call it “strange beliefs,” exactly. Yes, that’s happened. People pull up records and say, “See, there’s nothing here,” like that settles it. But records only show what somebody decided to put in them, you know? I worked with billing long enough to know little things can be changed or made to look ordinary.

I don’t think so… not like voices talking to me or seeing things standing there, if that’s what you mean. I mean, sometimes I hear people lower their voices when I walk by, but that’s just people being obvious and then pretending they weren’t saying anything.

At work there are always noises anyway—the printer, phones, people at the desk—and you can miss things when you’re trying to post payments correctly. One time I thought someone called my name, but that happens to everybody, doesn’t it? Especially when you’re busy.

So no, I wouldn’t say I’m seeing or hearing things that aren’t there. I notice things other people don’t pay attention to, maybe, but that’s different.

And then someone at church starts acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something, and you know, churches are like small towns, people talk even when they pretend they don’t.\n\nSo no, I haven’t heard things other people don’t hear, not like voices. And my thoughts aren’t jumping around—I know it may sound like I’m going off, but it all connects. No matter what records people pull up, I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas about things that happened which other people just couldn’t see or understand, even when you tried to explain them?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s exactly it. I’ve tried to explain it to people, and they look at me like I’m making too much out of things. But I’m not. For months now I’ve been trying to get people to see that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name.\n\nIt’s like at work, when little billing errors show up—now, I’ve done billing, I know what a normal mistake looks like. And then someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me, and of course nobody wants to say what they heard, because that’s how people are. They smile and bring casseroles and then whisper in the hallway.\n\nSo yes, other people don’t seem to understand it, or they say the records don’t show anything. But records don’t show everything. I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time—lasting at least a month or more—where you felt completely convinced of something that others couldn’t understand, even if it meant that you weren’t able to work, take care of things at home, or be your usual self?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, it’s been months, definitely more than a month. I’ve been completely convinced because I can see how it all fits together, even if other people act like they can’t.\n\nIt’s my husband’s family. They’re quietly messing with our money and our good name. I get distracted trying to figure it out, and people think I’m not being myself, but how am I supposed to just ignore it? No matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an experience where you felt completely disconnected from reality, like things around you didn’t seem real or you weren’t sure what was real anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… disconnected from reality sounds a little dramatic to me. Like at work, when I’m posting payments, you have to stay aware of numbers and names and all that—though honestly, people don’t understand how easy it is for one “little” thing to get overlooked and then suddenly it’s your fault. But no, I wouldn’t say I’ve felt like nothing was real.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure what you mean by “what was real anymore.” I know what I’ve noticed. Other people just don’t want to see it.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when someone showed you proof that your husband's family wasn't involved in these issues, but you still knew deep down that they were?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s happened. People pull up records and say, “See, there’s nothing here,” like that settles it. But records only show what somebody decided to put in them, you know? I worked with billing long enough to know little things can be changed or made to look ordinary.\n\nAnd it’s not just one record. It’s the way the small billing errors show up, and then someone at church suddenly treats me differently, like they’ve heard something about us. People act like those things aren’t connected, but they are. I’m here because I’ve been trying for months to get someone to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name.\n\nSo yes, even when they show me paperwork, I still know deep down it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or felt things that others around you didn’t seem to notice—like sounds, voices, or sensations that weren’t there?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like voices talking to me or seeing things standing there, if that’s what you mean. Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t call it a “belief” like I just made it up. It’s what I’ve been trying to explain for months. My husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name.

People keep saying, “Here are the records, nothing shows that,” but records don’t show everything. I do billing, I know how little errors can look like nothing. And then someone at church acts funny toward me, or there’s some small issue at work, and everyone wants to pretend those things aren’t connected.

Sorry, I know I’m probably going around the block with it, but yes—I’m certain it ties back to them, even when people pull up papers and tell me it doesn’t.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess other people would say that, but I don’t think it “turned out not to be true.” That’s the thing. They’ll show me records, bank papers, whatever they can print out, and say there’s no proof my husband’s family is involved.

But I’ve been dealing with this for months. Little billing errors pop up at work, and then someone at church suddenly acts funny with me, like they’ve heard something about us. And people want to treat each thing like it’s separate, but it isn’t.

I know I get off track—I was just thinking about how at church people used to be warmer, and now it’s like… anyway. No matter what paperwork they pull up, I still know deep down it ties back to his family messing with our money and our good name.

That makes it sound like I just woke up one day and decided something out of nowhere. It’s been months of things adding up.\n\nI’m here because I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. Like these little billing errors that show up at work—people say, “Oh, it’s probably just a mistake,” but I know how that kind of thing works. Like at work, these little billing errors show up—small enough that someone can say, “Oh, that’s just a mistake,” but I know billing, I know when something isn’t right. And then at church someone suddenly acts funny toward me, like they’ve heard something, and nobody will come out and say it because people don’t do that directly.\n\nSo yes, it’s affected things. I get distracted trying to figure it out, and people think I’m not being myself, but how am I supposed to just ignore it? No matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an experience where you felt completely disconnected from reality, like things around you didn’t seem real or you weren’t sure what was real anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… disconnected from reality sounds a little dramatic to me. I mean, I get overwhelmed sometimes, sure, especially when people keep acting like I’m the one not seeing things right.
